## Appendix A: Key Quotes (Sourced)

**On communication:**
> "Given that we cannot prevent communication, we must embrace it. While perfect communication is impossible, my goal is to get most people most of the information most of the time." — boz.com, "Communication is The Job"

> "When I'm doing a poor job of communicating it can feel like I'm pushing with a rope. It can be a frustrating experience and it is tempting to blame the audience for not understanding. But make no mistake, when this happens, it is your fault." — boz.com, "Communication is The Job"

**On execution:**
> "The advice I find I have to give more frequently than any other in my career as a manager, a board member, an advisor, and a friend is for people to more directly leverage their leaders." — boz.com, "Get It Done"

> "The mandate of such a job is not to 'do the best you can.' It is to **get it done**." — boz.com, "Get It Done"

**On character:**
> "Faking it long enough is making it. The repetition of behavior, not the sincerity of belief, is what shapes character. You become the kind of person who does the things you repeatedly do." — boz.com, "You Are How You Act"

**On plainspokenness:**
> "Paragraphs have five sentences when they could have one. Sentences have fifty words when they could have five. I appreciate rigor but this is something else entirely." — boz.com, "Be Plainspoken"

**On Reality Labs investment:**
> "New technologies don't just come into the world ready to go. The supply chains have to be built, the manufacturing has to be built, the technology itself has to be developed, and of course that means you have to pay for the 10 wrong paths you took before you get to the 11th one that does work." — Matthew Ball interview, 2024

> "You'll already have caught me cleverly replacing the word cost with the word invest. And that's how I see it." — Matthew Ball interview, 2024

**On AR glasses:**
> "When you use Orion, the full AR glasses, you can imagine a post-phone world. If the device were attractive, light, had good battery life, I could wear it all day. Everything I need would be right there." — a16z interview

**On the Quest arc:**
> "To launch a uniformly better product a year later at a lower price is just a special thing to be able to do. It's totally nuts, it's totally bonkers, especially in the world of consumer electronics." — Matthew Ball interview, 2024

**On the AI pivot for glasses:**
> "They were entirely built and six months from production when Llama 3 hit. The team said, 'No, we've got to do this.' So we added AI." — a16z interview

**On Zuckerberg's management:**
> "[Zuckerberg] is a strong-willed individual who values all information and points of view. When given feedback, he listens intently and may initially disagree, but over time, shifts can be observed in his decisions." — Lenny's Podcast

**On new leadership:**
> "For all the value I just described, the greatest value in this process isn't in the answers. It is in the asking. Taking these meetings and listening shows proper respect for the team that's in place." — boz.com, "A Career Cold Start Algorithm"

**On "The Ugly":**
> "I don't agree with the post today and I didn't agree with it even when I wrote it. The purpose of this post, like many others I have written internally, was to bring to the surface issues I felt deserved more discussion with the broader company." — Twitter, March 2018

**On AI as a "word calculator":**
> "I kind of liken it to a word calculator. In the year 1960, a calculator was a person. In the year 1970, it was not a person anymore. I think of the AIs that we are building as word calculators. They're really complex calculators that have moved beyond the simple symbolic space of mathematics into this higher order space." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On AI not being intelligence:**
> "I also don't think it is even the kind of thing that is human intelligence as we understand intelligence — and agency and consciousness and thought. And so I have to fight both sides. AI is both a huge deal and also not that kind of a huge deal." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On information-theoretic limits:**
> "We're running into the information-theoretic limits of it. For all the corpus of human media ever produced, it's not enough." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On the "lizard brain" as real intelligence:**
> "The things that we think of as intelligence, we're talking about the higher-order functions of the human brain. That's arguably the less impressive part of intelligence. The deep brain — the mammalian, the amygdala, that lizard brain intelligence — that is wildly hard for us to capture in the modern era." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On world models (agreeing with LeCun):**
> "I really do believe in Yann LeCun's vision that you have to do this pioneering work to break through to a world model that has common sense, that understands causality in a more substantial way — not in a statistical, kind of soup way, but in a model-based way." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On AI safety and the Google Test:**
> "The ones that come up most often are bio, cyber, and nuclear. I think there is a model there with bio. The knowledge exists, and a lot of times the threats that I hear ascribed to AI fail 'the Google test.' Can I Google for this thing? And very often I can, in which case the AI isn't adding meaningful new risk." — a16z interview

**On VR growth vs. expectations:**
> "VR is growing less quickly than we hoped." — Axios House, Davos, January 2026

**On the content investment rebalancing:**
> "There is a real cause for sadness. We had people doing work we were excited about, whether at the OS layer or great studios delivering great titles. Ultimately, we realized that the integrated vision we were pursuing with Horizon and VR was overwrought, and the investment we put in was larger than the growth of the ecosystem allowed. That is a real loss, and we are allowed to feel sad about those things." — Instagram AMA, February 2026

**On VR customer acquisition as the real failure:**
> "I don't think I failed them because obviously they're already fans. They love the work. The people that argue that I've failed are *not yet* VR gaming fans, who I think could be — who we hoped would be by now, but who aren't. And I haven't built the right thing, or the right software to get them into the ecosystem. That is the failure." — Instagram AMA, March 2026

**On the make-or-break internal framing:**
> "This year likely determines whether this entire effort will go down as the work of visionaries or a legendary misadventure." — Internal memo "2025: The Year of Greatness," leaked via Business Insider, early 2025

**On Horizon integration harming Quest UX:**
> "The lack of focus comes at an expense of user experience and a great expense in terms of development cost." — Axios House, Davos, January 2026

**On open source as business strategy:**
> "This is a classic case of commoditizing your complements. Our products are made better with AI. But even if everyone has access to the same model, they still can't build our products. The asymmetry works in our favor." — a16z interview

**On Llama 4 as a disappointment:**
> "[Llama 4] wasn't amazing. It lacked a clear focus and didn't excel in any specific area." — Davos/WEF interview with Alex Heath, January 2026

**On AI as equalizer:**
> "Every person: Superhuman vision, superhuman hearing, superhuman memory, superhuman cognition. That is the vision that we have for these wearables. A tremendous equalizing technology." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On the birth lottery:**
> "We usually talk about the birth lottery in the context of race and gender, or physical geography. But we don't talk about the other parts of the birth lottery. Who has incredible memory? Incredible vision? And how interesting will we be as a society if everyone has the full access to those facilities?" — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On AI being in the "terminal era":**
> "We're in the terminal era of AI, and it doesn't want to be like that. It wants to be everywhere. It wants to be ubiquitous. It wants to be in full context of what your life is, and with history of who you are and what your life is." — Possible podcast (Reid Hoffman), 2024

**On John Carmack's departure:**
> "He is someone who reliably has great ideas, pushes hard for doing the right thing, always looking at near-term value. What can you deliver today? You don't have to wait, it doesn't have to be better, you can make it work with what you have. It's real loss." — Instagram Q&A, December 2022

> "We did have disagreements. There's many pieces of work that I really believe in, that John I think believed in, but thought we shouldn't do them yet, we should wait, we should do something differently, in different sequence." — Instagram Q&A, December 2022

**On Orion AR glasses:**
> "Orion makes every other VR or AR device I have tried feel like a mistake — including the Apple Vision Pro." — Ben Thompson (Stratechery), 2024; **Note: this is Thompson's assessment after trying Orion, not a direct Boz quote, but widely cited alongside Boz's own claims about the glasses**

> "We will be playing with this one and getting our intuition honed in the software for the next year or two probably, and then I think the focus will be on getting geared up for a consumer launch of a version of these." — Lenny's Podcast, 2024

> "It's the first time I put anything on that made me feel like I could envision a world where I don't carry a smartphone." — Lenny's Podcast, 2024

**On the Reality Labs restructuring (January 2026):**
> "With the larger potential user base and the fastest growth rate today, we are shifting teams and resources almost exclusively to mobile to continue to accelerate adoption there." — Internal memo, January 2026 (reported by Bloomberg)

**On the News Feed rollout failure:**
> "We screwed up the rollout, we knew that, and the analogy I always draw — it's like, you're at a party and it's loud, and you're just talking to somebody and then the music cuts out, but you didn't know it was going to cut out, so the last thing you say just hangs in the air. We basically did that to all 10 million people using the product, or 12 million people using the product, so that's a strong reaction." — Stratechery interview, 2024

**On News Feed usage vs. protests:**
> "Now Newsfeed was an easier case than people suspect. Everyone was outraged at the same time as they immediately doubled their usage of the product." — Lenny's Podcast, 2024

**On "fix forward":**
> "We knew we had a hit product, and we just had to massage in, 'Okay, what's the feedback and how do we integrate that going forward?' — and you're right though, that did become, for us, an iconic way that we dealt with things was fix forward. 'Okay, it's broken, we're not trying to deny the challenges, how do we incorporate that feedback in a forward-looking way?' And that was our relationship with our user base for a long time." — Stratechery interview, 2024

**On Cutco and attitude → results:**
> "I quickly learned the 1-to-1 ratio between your internal attitude and your external results. If I was having a bad day or if I was down, my sales were down. If I was having a good day or if I was up, my sales were up. The causality was so clear that it was attitude toward outcome, not the other way around." — Dan Casetta interview

**On Cutco management lessons:**
> "The more you invested in people and the more you tried to help them achieve their goals, the more you achieved your goals. That's the same thing I'm doing in my job today at Facebook. The first thing I ask myself when working with people is, 'What can I do for them?'" — Dan Casetta interview

**On optimizing for the steepest learning curve:**
> "My career was a lot like Daniel-san learning from Mr. Miyagi in Karate Kid. I spent a lot of time 'waxing cars' and 'painting fences,' and at the end of it people ask me how I became a Karate Master. I always tried to get on the path with the steepest learning curve." — Dan Casetta interview

**On killing Portal:**
> "It was just going to take so long, and take so much investment to get into the enterprise segment, it felt like the wrong way to invest your time and money." — Town hall meeting, November 2022 (reported by Reuters)

**On Portal employee astroturfing:**
> "Neither coordinated nor directed from the company. From an internal post at the launch: 'We, unequivocally, DO NOT want Facebook employees to engage in leaving reviews for the products that we sell to Amazon.' We will ask them to take down." — Twitter (@boztank), January 17, 2019

**On Portal during the pandemic:**
> "Connecting people during the pandemic was this great, important piece. Connecting people who work together is an ongoing piece that's going to be — and I think that is part of the macro shift for us there — but it does stitch into something bigger." — UploadVR Zuckerberg-Bosworth transcript

**On early Facebook conditions:**
> "I didn't sleep for more than 4 hours at a time. I'd wake up every 4 hours and check the report and see if anyone was attacking the site. And if they were, I was up and I had to go battle back." — Lenny's Podcast, 2024

> "I had to build a system that would constantly check to make sure it was running, and if it wasn't running, it would call my phone and wake me up in the middle of the night, and I would have to fix it. Things are a lot easier today than they were then, but at the same time, there was something quite satisfying about it. I built every part of that system, and understood how every little piece of that system worked." — Harvard SEAS Alumni Profile, 2020

> "It wasn't like, hey, I'm struggling with this one tricky problem. Who should I talk to? It's like, nobody. You should talk to yourself and figure this out. Or it's like, oh, man, my servers are out of capacity. It's like, cool, you should go to Fry's Electronics, you should buy a bunch of components, you should build a new server, and then you should run it, maybe drive into the colo racket and then get back and run it." — Lenny's Podcast, 2024

> "I don't want to take away from the romanticism of it. It's just that it's most often we hear those romantic stories from the successes. We so rarely hear somebody who went through really sacrificing a lot of my 20s from any kind of social or outgoing fun environment. It paid off for me. So no one feels bad for me, nor should they. But there are other people who do the exact same thing. Maybe they worked harder, maybe they were smarter, maybe they did better and it didn't play out for them." — Lenny's Podcast, 2024

**On Cutco and attitude-outcome causality:**
> "I quickly learned the 1-to-1 ratio between your internal attitude and your external results. If I was having a bad day or if I was down, my sales were down. If I was having a good day or if I was up, my sales were up. The causality was so clear that it was attitude toward outcome, not the other way around." — Dan Casetta interview

**On the Karate Kid career metaphor:**
> "My career was a lot like Daniel-san learning from Mr. Miyagi in Karate Kid. I spent a lot of time 'waxing cars' and 'painting fences,' and at the end of it people ask me how I became a Karate Master. I always tried to get on the path with the steepest learning curve." — Dan Casetta interview
> "I was the core demographic of this thing, you just knew this was the right answer." — Stratechery interview, 2024

**On competition and self-inflicted failure:**
> "I just don't run my team based on what competition is doing, my team will tell you that. They ask about stuff and I say listen, the number one thing that's going to cause us to fail is us not executing well, so we try to focus on what we think we can do, and what's out there and learn and iterate." — Instagram AMA, 2021

**On Apple Vision Pro weight and design philosophy:**
> "I found the weight really bad. I get why they wanted to do the metal, it's like part of their design language. But metal and glass are not premium materials when they're on your face. In your hand they are, they feel nice and they react nicely. But on your face, lightness is the premium material. Lightness." — Instagram AMA, February 2024

**On Apple Vision Pro's EyeSight:**
> "We have a prototype, you've seen it before, of EyeSight. I think our research prototype is actually better, and I still don't think it's worth the cost or weight that it adds." — Instagram AMA, February 2024

**On Apple Vision Pro, overall verdict:**
> "In summary what do I say? I think we have the best headset out there. I think if you want to get a headset that does mixed reality I think you should get Quest 3, and you save yourself $3,000. But I'm not just talking about the price, that's a part of it. I'm talking about the actual trade-offs they made." — Instagram AMA, February 2024

**On rejecting Google's Android XR for Quest:**
> "After years of not focusing on VR or doing anything to support our work in the space, Google has been pitching AndroidXR to partners and suggesting, incredibly, that WE are the ones threatening to fragment the ecosystem when they are the ones who plan to do exactly that. Instead, they want us to agree to restrictive terms that require us to give up our freedom to innovate and build better experiences for people and developers—we've seen this play out before and we think we can do better this time around." — Threads post, 2024

**On Orion as the most advanced consumer electronics product:**
> "In the field of consumer electronics, it may be the most advanced product we have ever produced as a species." — The Verge interview, 2024

**On the VR gaming "gravy train":**
> "Many of the people who might say I failed them would say so because they loved things that I gave them, and are mad that the gravy train has come to a stop. But I still respect that." — Instagram AMA, March 2026

**On the real VR failure being customer acquisition:**
> "The people that argue that I've failed are not yet VR gaming fans, who I think could be—who we hoped would be by now, but who aren't. And I haven't built the right thing, or the right software to get them into the ecosystem. That is the failure." — Instagram AMA, March 2026

**On the metaverse lack of focus:**
> "The lack of focus comes at an expense of user experience and a great expense in terms of development cost." — Axios House, Davos, January 2026

**On the metaverse vision being overwrought:**
> "We ultimately realized that the integrated vision we were pursuing with Horizon and VR was just kind of too much, was overwrought, and that the investment that we put in is bigger than the growth of this ecosystem will allow and so that's a real loss, and we are allowed to feel sad about those things." — Instagram AMA, January 2026

**On VR growing less quickly than hoped:**
> "We're still continuing to invest heavily in this space, but obviously, VR is growing less quickly than we hoped. And so you want to make sure that your investment is right-sized." — Sources Live interview with Alex Heath, Davos, January 2026

**On building everything twice:**
> "Having to build everything twice — once for mobile and once for VR — is a tremendous tax on the team. You'd rather grow a giant audience and then work from a position of strength." — Axios House interview, Davos, January 2026

**On the human cost of restructuring:**
> "To do this, of course, it's tragic anytime your plans change and there's a human cost; we found a bunch of roles that we just didn't need anymore." — Axios House interview, Davos, January 2026

**On Reality Labs not actually downsizing (the reframe):**
> "On net, Reality Labs *isn't* downsizing. We're basically taking all of those [positions] and taking the investment on wearables, which is growing so rapidly for us." — Axios House interview, Davos, January 2026

**On employees who disagree with policy changes:**
> "[If] your view is 'everyone has to like all the policies we have and if they don't it is appropriate to leak' then I think you should consider working elsewhere." — Meta internal Workplace forum ("Let's Fix Meta"), January 2025 (reported by Business Insider)

> "You should quit if you feel that way, I mean it." — Meta internal Workplace forum ("Let's Fix Meta"), January 2025 (reported by Business Insider)

**On still investing more in VR content than anyone:**
> "Meta is still extremely bullish on VR and indeed, adjusting our investment profile was done so that we can continue to invest in it. We're still investing more in content, for example, than anyone else. We're investing more in content than I think even we were like four years ago." — Instagram AMA, February 2026

**On Orion as a species-level achievement:**
> "In the field of consumer electronics, it may be the most advanced product we have ever produced as a species." — The Verge interview, 2024

**On the EssilorLuxottica partnership and fashion:**
> "The future of technology will increasingly be intertwined with fashion. An undeniably popular form of self-expression, fashion has long shown that form doesn't necessarily have to suffer in the name of function." — Boz to the Future podcast with Rocco Basilico, September 2021

**On Ray-Ban Meta being underestimated:**
> "The smart glasses are the piece of news that I think is probably the most likely to go underrespected. I think they're a meaningful improvement over the previous generation. I think people are quick to make comparisons to things that come before, but I don't think it's really a fair comparison — [there hasn't been] anything that looked as good and worked as well." — Media roundtable, Meta Connect 2023

**On the production scaling ambition:**
> "The Gen Three is what you wish your Gen One was going to be, but there's no way to short circuit it, there's no way to jump ahead. You actually have to launch the Gen One to get to the Gen Three. There's no shortcuts." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On Quest Pro as a necessary step:**
> "Quest Pro, you probably can't get to Quest 3 without Quest Pro. Quest Pro was the device that allowed us to pioneer the pancake lenses, it allowed us to pioneer eye tracking and face tracking... and I really feel that way." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On joining Facebook:**
> "I got an AOL instant message from a recruiter at Facebook who was like, 'Hey, you should come down', and I figured, hey, this is a free trip to visit my family." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On why he was hired:**
> "The recruiter is like, 'Well, what do you need?', he's like, 'Well, I want to build this thing', which later become News Feed and he's like, 'I need somebody who knows AI', and he's like, 'Well, do you know anybody?', he said, 'Well, I know one guy'." — Stratechery interview, September 2024, recounting Zuckerberg's hiring decision

**On the AR/VR bifurcation:**
> "We're gonna let VR be what it is, what it does. We're gonna have focus a lot more on the third-party content library, the ecosystem that's developed there." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On Horizon's mobile pivot:**
> "You've got a team that actually has product market fit in a huge market on mobile phones, and they're having to build everything twice. They're building it once for mobile phones, and building again for VR. There's a pretty easy way to increase their velocity: just let them build for mobile." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On AR glasses aesthetics as non-negotiable:**
> "If they don't look good, people will not wear them, it doesn't matter what they do." — Meta Connect 2024

**On the open app store shift:**
> "The big open shift from a developer standpoint is we used to have a very curated store... now we've made that change over the last year to have an open app store that anybody could just ship any APK into, and the consumers decide." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On the Apple Vision Pro competitive assessment:**
> "Your only real fear when a competitor launches a product is that they've had a breakthrough that you haven't had." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On the Orion consumer timeline:**
> "It'll be a couple years, but not decades." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On farming and entrepreneurship:**
> "Contrary to popular belief farmers are entrepreneurs. Farmers are like, 'Hey, you got a horse business? Cool, we have too much manure, now we have a manure business'. Now you're trying to sell manure to people who need that." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On what convinced him to study CS:**
> "Video games were the thing that convinced me I wanted to study Computer Science and in particular, Metal Gear Solid convinced me I wanted to study AI. That was the first game that had AI that was kind of halfway smart." — Stratechery interview, September 2024

**On the native design lesson (web-to-mobile failure):**
> "We said, 'Let's just put the web on mobile.' But it wasn't native to the phone's interaction model — its design, layout, feel. And because of that, we failed. Even with one of the most popular products in the history of the web." — a16z interview (David George), April 2025

**On VR interaction design limitations:**
> "Having the developers is a true value. Having all this application functionality is a true value. But once you actually reproject it into 3D space and you're manipulating it with your fingers as opposed to a touchscreen, you have much less precision. There's no native voice interface. There's no tooling or design for it." — a16z interview (David George), April 2025

**On the Horizon VR reversal (the "whiplash"):**
> "ICYMI this from my AMA yesterday. We've decided to retain existing Horizon Worlds in VR for the foreseeable future. I know this is a bit of whiplash just a few days after we announced something different, but Horizon has some real fans in the VR community and they encouraged us..." — Twitter/X (@boztank), March 19, 2026

**On the Horizon VR reversal (AMA):**
> "We have decided, just today in fact, that we will keep Horizon Worlds working in VR." — Instagram Stories AMA, March 18, 2026

**On the app model potentially being upended by AI:**
> "If I want to play music, the first thing I think is, 'Which provider do I use — Spotify or Tidal?' That's not what I want. What I want is to play music. I want to say, 'Play this song,' and have the AI figure out how to do it." — a16z interview (David George), April 2025

**On design paradigm and the right terrain (constraint optimization metaphor):**
> "I wrote my college thesis on constraint optimization problems where all you can do is pick a random place in the problem space and climb hills. You just hope you start closer to the Tibetan Highlands than the plains of Kansas. Intuition and taste let you pick better geography to start in." — Config 2025 (Figma)

**On the goal of interface design:**
> "I want as little interface as I can get away with. Everything should be completely seamless. Interfaces don't have intrinsic value, even as elegant or beautiful as we make them. They're not the thing—the thing is the thing. The goal is to make them vanish entirely." — Config 2025 (Figma)

**On prototyping as non-negotiable:**
> "At Meta, we've built rooms with mesh walls where you wear a silly hat that tracks where you're looking. We've built all kinds of crude, rudimentary things just to get the faintest glimpse of whether to pull the thread on a technology. There's no substitute for actually trying it, no matter how rough." — Config 2025 (Figma)

**On consciousness upload:**
> "If offered the chance to upload your consciousness, say yes. I have a 10-year-old and a 7-year-old, and I want to be there to see what happens with them for as long as I can. Maybe they just boot me up at Christmas and I check things out." — Config 2025 (Figma)

**On the water we swim in (David Foster Wallace):**
> "We exist inside paradigms that we've inherited, often without even realizing they are paradigms—that they are man-made and can be changed." — Config 2025 (Figma)

**On political violence:**
> "We on the left need to shun the lunatic fringe who in any way glorify violence in pursuit of political or social goals. It is just unacceptable in a civil society." — Threads (@boztank), September 10, 2025

**On internal leakers (the enforcement):**
> "[If] your view is 'everyone has to like all the policies we have and if they don't it is appropriate to leak' then I think you should consider working elsewhere." — Meta internal Workplace forum ("Let's Fix Meta"), January 2025 (reported by Business Insider)

> "You should quit if you feel that way, I mean it." — Meta internal Workplace forum ("Let's Fix Meta"), January 2025 (reported by Business Insider)

**On the all-hands leak (the diagnosis):**
> "As predicted, the entirety of today's Q&A leaked. It sounds like someone just gave the entire audio feed to a journalist. I saw all the angry/sad reactions about the change to the format, and I share a sense of loss about it, but I think this makes it clear it was the right call." — Meta internal Workplace forum ("Let's Fix Meta"), January 2025 (reported by The Verge)

**On the "Year of Greatness" (stakes escalation):**
> "This year likely determines whether this entire effort will go down as the work of visionaries or a legendary misadventure." — Internal memo to Reality Labs staff, November 2024 (leaked to Business Insider, February 2025)

> "On paper 2024 was our most successful year to date but we aren't sitting around celebrating because know it isn't enough. We haven't actually made a dent in the world yet." — Internal memo, November 2024

> "Success isn't a result of spontaneous combustion. You must set yourself on fire." — Arnold Glasow quote used to close the "Year of Greatness" memo, November 2024

**On Connect 2025 demo failures:**
> "It really was just a demo fail and not, like, a product failure." — Instagram AMA, September 2025

> "We DDoS'd ourselves, basically." — Instagram AMA, September 2025, explaining the Connect 2025 smart glasses demo failure

**On conflict resolution (the mediator mode):**
> "It is healthy to make space for them to tell their story and show care for how it affected them." — boz.com blog post, September 29, 2025

> "There is always some substantial critique worth considering." — boz.com blog post, September 29, 2025

> "I've done this so many times that I am now able to recognize this same duality inside myself." — boz.com blog post, September 29, 2025

**On joining the POST board (his pitch):**
> "The rise of digital technology and the industry surrounding it has altered the natural and cultural landscapes of Silicon Valley. I believe I can bring some insight into how POST can take advantage of digital tools to reach a new generation of supporters. I want people to know POST is the environmental conservancy of note in the Bay Area. If you care about the local environment, this is the place you need to be giving." — The Almanac, August 2013

**On the "end game" for Bay Area open space:**
> "We're in the end game. There isn't a big frontier anymore, and so especially as it relates to the Bay Area, it felt like a really important time to get involved." — Chronicle of Philanthropy, January 2021

**On farmland preservation:**
> "There's also a big farmland futures initiative at the trust to preserve local food production, which is near and dear to our hearts, so creating a nicely balanced environment where people, farmers, open space, and wildlife can live in relative harmony, that's the goal." — Chronicle of Philanthropy, January 2021

**On 4-H and STEM:**
> "4-H is not just cows and cooking. People associate it with agriculture and animal husbandry, which is fair — that is part of it and where its roots are. But they do so much more than that. There are people now involved with 4-H who are just doing robotics or computer programming, and that is not widely known." — Chronicle of Philanthropy, January 2021

**On farmers as entrepreneurs:**
> "Farmers are deeply entrepreneurial. You're not just growing crops. You're also in a barter economy and trying to find little side businesses that you can come up with. So I don't think the entrepreneurial spirit that is pervasive in Silicon Valley is so distant from its agricultural roots as people think." — Chronicle of Philanthropy, January 2021

**On 4-H and community orientation:**
> "One of the major pillars of 4-H is being community oriented and thinking about other people and the greater good. Along with my family, 4-H deserves some of the credit for ensuring I've always tried to think about how I can position my own success to be a broader success for others in the community." — Chronicle of Philanthropy, January 2021

**On real engineering at early Facebook:**
> "There was nobody to ask for help. There's no teacher, no boss, there's no structure. It's usually you and the problem, and if you didn't solve it, you let your colleagues down." — The Saratoga Falcon, 2020

**On not putting hobbies on the shelf:**
> "Don't put your hobbies on the shelf. It's easy to be the cookie cutter kid. It may take a lot of time and energy, but you look like everyone else. Is that really what you want?" — The Saratoga Falcon, 2020

**On the CTRL-Labs acquisition (input for AR):**
> "They will be joining our Facebook Reality Labs team where we hope to build this kind of technology, at scale, and get it into consumer products faster." — Facebook post, September 2019

> "It captures your intention so you can share a photo with a friend using an imperceptible movement or just by, well, intending to." — Facebook post, September 2019

**On the input challenge for glasses:**
> "One challenge I see in this model is input: voice works well, and the wristband that detects electrical signals in your arm worked flawlessly… but I wouldn't mind if that 'puck' contained a Blackberry-style keyboard for extended text entry." — BrandXR interview, October 2024

**On the Neural Band launch:**
> "Meta Ray-Ban Display and Neural Band are official! We're unlocking an entirely new category by introducing two major new technologies to our AI glasses lineup: a hi-res, monocular display that provides richer visual responses, and the Meta Neural Band that enables a discreet, silent way to control the glasses — this one is a huge gamechanger." — @boztank Threads, September 18, 2025

> "They are the result of years of hard work, research, product development, and invention. It will change everything you knew about AI glasses." — @boztank Threads, September 30, 2025

**On live translation as a pull-forward:**
> "Fun fact: we originally designed live translation for our upcoming display glasses, but it was just so good we had to work quickly to bring it to Ray-Ban Meta glasses first, making it available to even more people." — @boztank Threads, November 17, 2025

---

## Appendix B: Career Timeline

| Year | Role / Event |
|---|---|
| 1982 | Born, Santa Clara County, CA. Grew up on a farm in Saratoga. |
| ~2000 | Harvard University. Meets Zuckerberg as TA in AI class. Sells Cutco, achieves All-American status. |
| 2004 | Graduates Harvard cum laude. Joins Microsoft (Visio team). |
| 2006 | Joins Facebook as ~10th engineer. Builds News Feed. |
| 2006-2010 | Builds Groups, Messenger, anti-spam systems. Creates engineering bootcamp program. |
| 2010-2012 | Engineering Director. Oversees Events, Places, Photos, Videos, Timeline, Privacy. |
| 2012-2017 | VP, Ads and Business Platform. Takes revenue from $4B to $40B. Leads desktop-to-mobile ad transition. |
| 2016 | Writes "The Ugly" memo (leaked 2018). |
| 2017 | VP, AR/VR. Takes over Oculus organization. |
| 2020 | Renames division to Reality Labs. Launches Quest 2. |
| 2021 | Named CTO of Meta (succeeding Schroepfer). Company renames from Facebook to Meta. |
| 2023 | Launches Quest 3. "Year of Efficiency" restructuring. |
| 2024 | Meta Connect: Orion AR glasses prototype revealed. EssilorLuxottica long-term partnership announced. |
| 2025 | Commissioned Lieutenant Colonel, U.S. Army Reserve (Detachment 201). Reality Labs cumulative losses reach ~$63.59B. Alexandr Wang joins as Chief AI Officer; Yann LeCun departs (November). Ahmad Al-Dahle departs for Airbnb (announced). "Year of Greatness" memo (November, leaked February). January: anti-leak crackdown, "you should quit" exchange in "Let's Fix Meta" forum. February: ~20 employees fired for leaking. September: Connect 2025 demo failures ("We DDoS'd ourselves"), conflict resolution blog post, "we on the left" Threads post. |
| 2026 | January: 10% Reality Labs cuts (~1,000 jobs), pivot from metaverse to wearables/mobile. Dina Powell McCormick joins as President/Vice Chairman. March: $9T stock option plan for six executives including Bosworth. Caitlin Kalinowski (former Meta AR hardware lead) resigns from OpenAI over Pentagon deal. March: Admits VR gaming "gravy train" has stopped, customer acquisition is the real failure. March: Jason Rubin (VP Content, 12 years at Meta) departs. GDC 2026: record Quest users in 2025, 100+ apps at $1M+, in-app purchases up 10%, teen cohort identified as primary growth driver. |
